{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}_{t}
at
\textstyle\bf y
, then so does
\max\{f_{1},...,f_{k}\}
, so
\max\{f_{1},...,f_{k}\}
was not removed from
S
, as desired. Furthermore, if
f_{1},...,f_{k}
are taken to be the functions guaranteed by Item 2, Lemma
17
implies that
\max\{f_{1},...,f_{k}\}
has optimal value greater than
\textup{OPT}+\varepsilon
, so since we just showed
\max\{f_{1},...,f_{k}\}\in S_{t}
, the remainder of item 3 follows.

If
t=\ell
,
S_{t}
contains the single function
f_{\max}
, which has optimal value greater than
\textup{OPT}+\varepsilon
by its construction as a consequence of Lemma
17
. Hence, Item 3 follows.
To prove item 1, we will use that
S_{t-1}
satisfies Item 1, and by Item 3 applied to
S_{t-1}
,
f_{\max}
is consistent with the responses returned by the procedure up to round
t-1
. For round
t
itself, consistency follows from the definition of
v_{t}
and
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}_{t}
, and so
f_{\max}
is consistent with all responses given. Item 2 does not apply in this case and Item 4 is immediate by the construction of
S_{t}:=\{f_{\max}\}
.

If
t>\ell
, then
S^{t}=S^{t-1}=\{f_{\max}\}
and it suffices to check that the response
(v_{t},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}_{t})
is compatible with
f_{\max}
, which follows immediately from the definition of the response.

Hence, Items 1-4 of the lemma follow. It remains to show that
Adv-Cont+
is indeed a well-defined adversary under a full-information first-order oracle. For rounds
t\leq\ell-1
, this is inherited from
Adv-Cont
, while for
t\geq\ell
, this is ensured because if the queried point
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}_{t}
is the same as
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}_{t^{\prime}}
for some round
t^{\prime}<t
,
Adv-Cont+
provides the same response in round
t
as in round
t^{\prime}
. Thus, there is indeed a first-order chart
\mathcal{G}
(derived from the first order map
\mathcal{G}_{0}
for
Adv-Cont
) such that
Adv-Cont+
is an adversary under the corresponding full-information first-order oracle
\mathcal{O}(\mathcal{G})
.∎

2.2.2
Constructing the mixed-integer adversary
Adv-MI

We now construct the family
\mathcal{F}_{MI}
of functions over
\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
used to transfer the lower bound to the mixed-integer setting, along with the adversary
Adv-MI
for that family. We call functions over
\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}

full-dimensional
to distinguish them from the functions over
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, the continuous part of the problem.
As indicated previously, these full-dimensional functions
\psi
in
\mathcal{F}_{MI}
will be obtained by considering combinations of selecting one function
f_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}}
from
\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 1.52779pt\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{F}$}}_{cont}
for each of the mixed-integer fibers
\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d},\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}\in\{0,1\}^{n}
, letting
\psi
equal the appropriate function selected over each corresponding fiber,
and applying an interpolation scheme between the fibers. This interpolation is illustrated in Figure
1
and described in detail later in this section.

For the behavior of
Adv-MI
, we instantiate a copy of the modified continuous adversary
Adv-Cont+
on each fiber.
Whenever the optimization algorithm queries a point
(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}})
on a fiber, we send
\textstyle\bf y
to the continuous adversary on the fiber and report back the response
(v,{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}})
received, although
\textstyle\bf g
needs to be appropriately lifted to the full
\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
space to be consistent with the way we interpolate the functions between the fibers.
If the optimization algorithm only probes on these fibers, then it is intuitive that such an adversary would be
\varepsilon
-hard for
2^{n}\ell-1
rounds:
informally, up to this round, at least one of the
2^{n}
fibers that has received no more than
\ell-1
queries, so using the hardness of
Adv-Cont+
(Item 2 of Lemma
16
) we can obtain full-dimensional functions that do not share an
\varepsilon
-approximate solution, which confirm the desired
\varepsilon
-hardness of the mixed integer adversary
Adv-MI
.

The crucial element is how to deal with queries on points outside of the mixed-integer fibers. If such queries provide the algorithm with more information about the full-dimensional functions
\mathcal{F}_{MI}
than queries on the fibers do, then we may not have full-dimensional functions with no common
\varepsilon
-approximate solution surviving for
2^{n}\ell-1
rounds. To handle this issue, the interpolation used to define the full-dimensional functions
\psi
guarantees that its behavior on a fractional point
(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}})\notin\{0,1\}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
is completely determined by the value of the function
f_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}})
from
\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 1.52779pt\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{F}$}}_{cont}
selected for the fiber
\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
, where
\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}\in\{0,1\}^{n}
is the closest 0/1 point to
\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}
. Thus,
Adv-MI
can also answer such a query at a fractional point by making a query to the appropriate continuous adversary
Adv-Cont+
on
\left\{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}\right\}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and the hardness of the latter can still be leveraged.

We now formally define the functions
\mathcal{F}_{MI}
and the adversary
Adv-MI
.

Figure 1
:
(Left) Illustration of two possible functions
f_{0},f_{1}\in\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 1.03743pt\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{F}$}}_{cont}
(blue and red) of the continuous adversary
Adv-Cont+
, for
d=1
. (Right) Illustration of the function
\psi_{(f_{0},f_{1})}
for the mixed-integer adversary
Adv-MI
obtained by placing the functions
f_{0}
and
f_{1}
on the fibers
\{0\}\times\mathbb{R}
and
\{1\}\times\mathbb{R}
and interpolating appropriately between the fibers.

Construction of the functions
\mathcal{F}_{MI}
.

For a 0/1 point
\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}\in\{0,1\}^{n}
and a function
f\in\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 1.52779pt\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{F}$}}_{cont}
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
we first define its (convex) extension to the full-dimensional space
\mathbb{R}^{n+d}
as

\displaystyle\hat{f}_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}})=\max\Big\{f({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}})+\langle\mathbf{M}_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}-\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}\rangle~,~\textup{OPT}\Big\},

(2)

with
\mathbf{M}_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}}:=3MR\cdot sgn(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}-0.5\cdot\mathbf{1}),
where
sgn
denotes the sign function. This construction effectively places
f
along the
\textstyle\bf y
space at the fiber
\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}
and extends it in each of the
\textstyle\bf x
variables via a linear function with slope
\pm 3MR
, in a way that it decreases the value as it moves into the unit cube, or equivalently, away from
\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}
; it then truncates the final value to being at least
OPT
; see Figure
2
for an illustration.
We note for later use that wherever the extension is not truncated by
OPT
, a subgradient is given by appending the vector
\mathbf{M}_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}}
to a subgradient of
f
, and otherwise the all zeroes vector is a subgradient. More precisely, we have

\displaystyle\partial\hat{f}_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}})\supseteq\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\{\mathbf{M}_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}}\}\times\partial f({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}})&\textrm{, if $\hat{f}_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}})>\textup{OPT}$}\\
\{\bm{0}\}&\textrm{, otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

Figure 2
:
An example of a possible function from
f\in\mathcal{F}_{cont}
(left) together with an illustration of its truncated extension
\hat{f}_{0}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}})
(right) as constructed in (
2
).

Given a collection
\texttt{F}=(f_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}})_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}}
with one function
f_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}}\in\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 1.52779pt\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{F}$}}_{cont}
for each 0/1 point
\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}
, we combine them into the convex function

\displaystyle\psi_{\texttt{F}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}):=\max_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}\in\{0,1\}^{n}}\hat{f}_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}),

(5)

where we abuse notation slightly and write the convex extension
(\widehat{f_{\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}})_{\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}
as simply
\hat{f}_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}}
to simplify the notation.
As mentioned above, a crucial property of these functions is that their behavior between the fibers is determined by the behavior on the closest fiber. Intuitively, the slope
\pm 3MR
guarantees that as the
\textstyle\bf x
argument moves away from the base fiber
\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}
of each extended function
\hat{f}_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}}
,
\hat{f}_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}}
decreases rapidly enough so that the maximum in (
5
) is always achieved by the extended function at the closest fiber to
\textstyle\bf x
. Figure
1
illustrates this, where one can see that both functions placed on the fibers get fully truncated in between the fibers. To make this precise, let
r({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}):[0,1]^{n}\rightarrow\{0,1\}^{n}
map any
\textstyle\bf x
in the box to its closest 0/1 point in
\ell_{\infty}
-norm, that is
r({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}):=\argmin_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\prime}}\{\|{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}:{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}^{n}\}
.

Lemma 18
.

For every collection
\texttt{F}=(f_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}})_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}}
, for every point
({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}})\in[0,1]^{n}\times[-R,R]^{d}
we have

\displaystyle\psi_{\texttt{F}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}})=\hat{f}_{r({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}})}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}),~~~~\textrm{ and }~~~~\partial\psi_{\texttt{F}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}})=\partial\hat{f}_{r({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}})}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}})

Proof.

Define
B_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}}:=\left\{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:\|{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\prime}-\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{3}\right\}
for every
\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\in\{0,1\}^{n}
. Consider an arbitrary
({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}})\in[0,1]^{n}\times[-R,R]^{d}
.

Case 1:
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\not\in B_{\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}
for any
\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\in\{0,1\}^{n}
.
This implies that for every
\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\in\{0,1\}^{n}
, we have

\displaystyle f_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}})+\langle\mathbf{M}_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}-\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}\rangle

\displaystyle=f_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}})+\sum_{j\in[n]}3MR\cdot sgn(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}_{j}-0.5)\cdot({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}_{j}-\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}_{j})

\displaystyle\leq f_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}})-3MR\cdot\max_{j\in[n]}|{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}_{j}-\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}_{j}|<f_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}})-MR\leq\textup{OPT},

Thus,
\hat{f}_{\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}})=\textup{OPT}
for all
\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\in\{0,1\}^{n}
. As a result,
\psi_{F}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}})=\textup{OPT}=\hat{f}_{r({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}})}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}})
.

Moreover, since
f_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}})+\langle\mathbf{M}_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}-\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}\rangle<\textup{OPT}
for all
\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\in\{0,1\}^{n}
, there exists a neighborhood of
({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}})
such that for any point
({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\prime},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}^{\prime})
in the neighborhood, it holds that
f_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}^{\prime})+\langle\mathbf{M}_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\prime}-\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}\rangle<\textup{OPT}
, and
\hat{f}_{\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\prime},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}^{\prime})=\textup{OPT}
for all
\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\in\{0,1\}^{n}
. As a result,
\partial\hat{f}_{\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}})=\{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 0$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 0$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 0$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 0$}}}\}
for all
\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\in\{0,1\}^{n}
, and
\partial\psi_{F}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}})=\{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 0$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 0$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 0$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 0$}}}\}.

Case 2:
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\in B_{\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}
for some
\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\in\{0,1\}^{n}
.
In this case,
r({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}})=\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}
. This is because for any
\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\in\{0,1\}^{n}\backslash\{\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\}
, we have that

\displaystyle\|{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}-\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\|_{\infty}\geq\frac{2}{3}>\frac{1}{3}\geq\|{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}-\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}\|_{\infty}.

(6)

It is also true that
\hat{f}_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}})\geq\textup{OPT}=\hat{f}_{\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}})
for any
\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\neq\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}
, which holds due to the result from Case 1.

Moreover, the arguments from Case 1 and (
6
) imply that there exists a neighborhood of
({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}})
such that for any point
({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\prime},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}^{\prime})
in the neighborhood, it holds that
r({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\prime})=r({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}})=\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}
, and
\hat{f}_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\prime},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}^{\prime})\geq\textup{OPT}=\hat{f}_{\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\prime},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}^{\prime})
for all
\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\neq\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}
. As a result,
\psi_{F}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\prime},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}^{\prime})=\hat{f}_{\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\prime},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}^{\prime})=\hat{f}_{r({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\prime})}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\prime},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}^{\prime})=\hat{f}_{r({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}})}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{\prime},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}^{\prime})
and
\partial\psi_{F}(x,y)=\partial\hat{f}_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}})=\partial\hat{f}_{r({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}})}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}).

∎

Construction of the mixed-integer adversary
Adv-MI
.

We finally describe
Adv-MI
in Procedure
2
. Its main property is captured in the following invariant.

Procedure 2
.

Adv-MI

Instantiate a copy of
Adv-Cont+
on each fiber
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\in\{0,1\}^{n}
, and let
S({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}})
denote the set of surviving functions maintained in every round by this copy, initialized to
\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{cont}
.

For each round
t=1,2\ldots
:

1.

Adv-MI
receives the query
({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}_{t},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}_{t})
from the algorithm. Send
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}_{t}
to the adversary
Adv-Cont+
associated with the closest fiber
r({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}_{t})
, which then returns a value
v
and subgradient
\textstyle\bf g
, and updates its maintained set of surviving functions
S(r({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}_{t}))
of its fiber
r({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}_{t})
.

2.

Adv-MI
returns as its response to the query
({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}_{t},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}_{t})
the value

\displaystyle\tilde{v}_{t}=\max\Big\{v+\langle\mathbf{M}_{r({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}_{t})},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}_{t}-r({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}_{t})\rangle~,~\textup{OPT}\Big\},

and as subgradient returns either
\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}}_{t}=(\mathbf{M}_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}})
or
\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}}_{t}=\bm{0}
depending whether
\tilde{v}_{t}>\textup{OPT}
or not (i.e., whether
\hat{f}_{r({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}})}
was truncated at
({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}_{t},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}_{t})
or not), respectively.

Invariant 1
.

There exists a first order chart
\mathcal{G}
(derived from
\mathcal{G}_{0}
) such that, for any algorithm, the sets
S({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}})
,
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\in\{0,1\}^{n}
maintained by
Adv-MI
satisfy the following property.

In every round, for every collection
\texttt{F}=(f_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\in\{0,1\}^{n}}
of current surviving functions
f_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}\in S({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}})
for
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\in\{0,1\}^{n}
, the function
\psi_{\texttt{F}}
is consistent with the response returned by
Adv-MI
under the full-information first-order oracle
\mathcal{O}(\mathcal{G})
, i.e.,
\psi_{\texttt{F}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}_{t},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}_{t})=\tilde{v}_{t}
and
\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}}_{t}\in\partial\psi_{\texttt{F}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}_{t},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}_{t})
.

Notice that Invariant
1
is indeed maintained after each response in Step 2 of Procedure
2
: For every collection
\texttt{F}=(f_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}})_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}}
of still surviving functions
f_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}}\in S(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})
, by the consistency guarantee of
Adv-Cont+
(Item 1 of Lemma
16
) the function
f_{r({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}_{t})}
selected for the fiber
r({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}_{t})
has value
v
and subgradient
\textstyle\bf g
at
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}_{t}
;
thus, Lemma
18
combined with (
2
) implies that the function
\psi_{\texttt{F}}
has value

\psi_{\texttt{F}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}_{t},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}_{t})=\hat{f}_{r({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}})}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}_{t},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}_{t})=\max\Big\{f_{r({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}_{t})}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}_{t})+\langle\mathbf{M}_{r({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}_{t})},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}_{t}-r({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}_{t})\rangle~,~\textup{OPT}\Big\}=\tilde{v}_{t},

and similarly from (
2.2.2
) we see that
\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}}
is a subgradient in
\partial\psi_{\texttt{F}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}_{t},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}_{t})=\partial\hat{f}_{r({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}_{t})}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}_{t},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}_{t})

, as desired.

We now prove that
Adv-MI
is
\varepsilon
-hard for
2^{n}\ell-1
rounds; using Lemma
15
, this implies Theorem
7
for the case of full-information first-order oracle. Suppose the optimization algorithm runs for fewer than
2^{n}\cdot\ell
iterations. Then there is a fiber
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*}\in\{0,1\}
where
Adv-MI
sent at most
\ell-1
queries to the adversary
Adv-Cont+
of the fiber
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*}
.
Thus, by the guarantee of the latter (Item 2 of Lemma
16
), the surviving set
S({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*})
has some finite collection of functions
f^{1}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*}},...,f^{k}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*}}
with no common
\varepsilon
-approximate solution.
Consider the collections
\texttt{F}^{1},...,\texttt{F}^{k}
of surviving functions that have
f^{1}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*}},...,f^{k}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*}}
, respectively, for the fiber
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*}
and any function
f_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}}\in S(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}})
with optimal value
>\textup{OPT}+\varepsilon
for each of the other fibers
\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}\neq{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*}
, which exist on each of the other fibers by Item 3 of Lemma
16
. By Invariant
1
, all functions
\psi_{\texttt{F}^{1}},...,\psi_{\texttt{F}^{k}}
are compatible with the responses returned by
Adv-MI
. The desired
\varepsilon
-hardness of
Adv-MI
then follows from the following claim, which then concludes the proof.

Claim 19
.

The functions
\psi_{\texttt{F}^{1}},...,\psi_{\texttt{F}^{k}}
share no common
\varepsilon
-approximate solution.

Proof.

From the construction above, we have that
\texttt{F}^{\dagger}:=\texttt{F}^{1}\backslash\{f^{1}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*}}\}=\texttt{F}^{2}\backslash\{f^{2}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*}}\}...=\texttt{F}^{k}\backslash\{f^{k}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*}}\}
.
Due to (
5
) and the definitions of
\texttt{F}^{1},...,\texttt{F}^{k}
, for any fiber
\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}\neq{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*}
and
f_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}}\in\texttt{F}^{\dagger}
, it follows that
\psi_{\texttt{F}^{1}}(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}})=...=\psi_{\texttt{F}^{k}}(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}})=f_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}})>\textup{OPT}+\varepsilon
. Thus, the
\varepsilon
-approximate solutions for the functions
\psi_{\texttt{F}^{1}},...,\psi_{\texttt{F}^{k}}
only exist within the fiber
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*}
. Given that the
\varepsilon
-approximate solutions of
f^{1}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*}},...,f^{k}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*}}
are disjoint, and considering that
\psi_{\texttt{F}^{j}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}})=f^{j}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}})
for all
j\in[k]
, we can conclude our proof.
∎

2.3
Proof of Theorem
7
for general oracles

We now prove Theorem
7
in full generality. We will do this using the exact same family of difficult functions
\psi_{\texttt{F}}
from (
5
), and also with the same idea of constructing a mixed-integer adversary that produces its answers by making queries to an adversary for the continuous problems on the fibers. Since the mixed-integer adversary will need to answer queries made in the full
\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
space by making queries in the continuous space
\mathbb{R}^{d}
on each fiber, we will require that the set of permissible queries that can be made to the continuous adversary is, in some sense, as rich as the queries allowed in the full space. For example, if one allows full-information queries to be made in
\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
, but only binary queries to be made in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
to the continuous adversary, one would struggle to determine how the mixed-integer adversary should answer those full-information queries by making only binary queries to the adversaries for the continuous subproblems.
Specifically, for a query at
({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
, knowing how
\textstyle\bf x
affects the function values and subgradients of
\psi_{\texttt{F}}
, the mixed-integer adversary needs to be able to determine what response to give by making a suitably chosen query about
f_{r({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}})}
to the continuous adversary.
We formalize this requirement of having the same richness of queries for the continuous subproblems as for the full
\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
space with the concept of
hereditary queries
.

Hereditary queries.

For simplicity, we define the notion of hereditary queries for unconstrained problems (i.e., only for value/subgradient queries), but we remark that the same idea can be applied to separation queries as well.

Definition 20
.

Let
\{\mathcal{H}_{n,d}^{\textup{val}}\}_{n,d\in{\mathbb{N}}}
and
\{\mathcal{H}_{n,d}^{\textup{sub}}\}_{n,d\in{\mathbb{N}}}
be classes of permissible function value and subgradient queries, respectively, with response sets (codomains)
H_{n,d}^{\textup{val}}
and
H_{n,d}^{\textup{sub}}
.
\{\mathcal{H}_{n,d}^{\textup{val}}\}_{n,d\in{\mathbb{N}}}
and
\{\mathcal{H}_{n,d}^{\textup{sub}}\}_{n,d\in{\mathbb{N}}}
are said to be
hereditary
if the following holds for all
n,d\in{\mathbb{N}}
and functions
\mathcal{M}:\{0,1\}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}
.
For any
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\in\{0,1\}^{n}
,
\delta\in\mathbb{R}
,
h^{\textup{val}}\in\mathcal{H}_{n,d}^{\textup{val}}
, and
h^{\textup{sub}}\in\mathcal{H}_{n,d}^{\textup{sub}}
,
there exists
h_{*}^{\textup{val}}\in\mathcal{H}_{0,d}^{\textup{val}}
,

h_{*}^{\textup{sub}}\in\mathcal{H}_{0,d}^{\textup{sub}}
and functions
B^{\textup{val}}:H_{0,d}^{\textup{val}}\to H_{n,d}^{\textup{val}}
,
B^{\textup{sub}}:H_{0,d}^{\textup{sub}}\to H_{n,d}^{\textup{sub}}

such that

\displaystyle B^{\textup{val}}(h_{*}^{\textup{val}}(v))

\displaystyle=h_{\textup{val}}(v+\delta)\qquad\forall v\in\mathbb{R},

(7)

\displaystyle B^{\textup{sub}}(h_{*}^{\textup{sub}}(v,{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}))

\displaystyle=h_{\textup{sub}}(\mathcal{M}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}),{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}})\hskip 5.69054pt\forall{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}

(8)

Intuitively, a class of queries being hereditary has the consequence that if for a point
({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
, one knows exactly the
\textstyle\bf x
component
\mathcal{M}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}})
of the subgradient, then one can simulate a query in the
\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
space by only making a query on the
\mathbb{R}^{d}
space, and similarly that there are queries rich enough to consider shifted function values
v+\delta
, where the interpretation is that
\delta
is the effect
\textstyle\bf x
has on the overall function value – see (
2
).

Example 21
.

We show that natural premissible queries, as from Definition
5
, are hereditary. Let
\mathcal{M}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}),\delta
be as in Definition
20
.

1.

(Full-information first-order oracle) If
\mathcal{H}_{n,d}^{\textup{val}}
and
\mathcal{H}_{n,d}^{\textup{sub}}
are simply the identity functions, then we can take
B^{\textup{val}}
to be
B^{\textup{val}}(v)=v+\delta
and take
B^{\textup{sub}}
to be the “lifting/rotation” map
B^{\textup{sub}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}})=(\mathcal{M}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}),{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}})
, noting that
h^{\textup{sub}},~h^{\textup{val}},~h_{*}^{\textup{sub}}
, and
h_{*}^{\textup{val}}
are all the identity functions.

2.

(General binary oracle) For the general binary oracle based on a first-order chart
\mathcal{G}
,
B^{\textup{val}}
and
B^{\textup{sub}}
can be taken to be the identity map from
\{0,1\}
to
\{0,1\}
, and one can take
h_{*}^{\textup{val}}(v)=h(v+\delta),~h_{*}^{\textup{sub}}=h(\mathcal{M}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}),{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}),
which are permissible queries since all binary queries are permissible.

3.

(Shifted bit oracle) If
\mathcal{H}_{n,d}^{\textup{val}}
and
\mathcal{H}_{n,d}^{\textup{sub}}
are from a shifted bit oracle
\mathcal{H}^{bit^{*}}
, then
B^{\textup{val}}
can be taken to be the identity map from
\{0,1\}
to
\{0,1\}
. For a query on the function value, if
h^{\textup{val}}
reports some bit of
v+\delta
, then the appropriate hereditary query is exactly the query
h_{*}^{\textup{val}}
such that
h_{*}^{\textup{val}}(v)=h(v+\delta)
, i.e. using the shift
u=\delta
in the notation of Definition
5
. A subgradient bit query
h^{\textup{sub}}(\mathcal{M}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}),{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}})
returns a bit of either
\mathcal{M}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}})
or
\textstyle\bf g
, so there are two cases.

i)

h^{\textup{sub}}
returns the
j^{th}
bit of the
k^{th}
entry of
\mathcal{M}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}})
. Set
B^{\textup{sub}}(\cdot)
to return exactly that bit of
\mathcal{M}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}})
, no matter the input to
B^{\textup{sub}}
, so
h_{*}^{\textup{sub}}
may be chosen arbitrarily.

ii)

h^{\textup{sub}}
returns the
j^{th}
bit of the
k^{th}
entry of
\textstyle\bf g
. Set
B^{\textup{sub}}
to be the identity map from
\{0,1\}
to
\{0,1\}
, and set
h_{*}^{\textup{sub}}
to return the desired bit of
\textstyle\bf g
.

4.

(Inner product threshold queries) If
\mathcal{H}_{n,d}^{\textup{val}}
and
\mathcal{H}_{n,d}^{\textup{sub}}
consist of the inner product threshold queries, take both
B^{\textup{val}}
and
B^{\textup{sub}}
to be the identity maps. For function value queries
h^{\textup{val}}_{u,c}(v)=sgn(u\cdot(v+\delta)-c)
, use

h_{*}^{\textup{val}}(v):=h_{u,c-u\delta}^{\textup{val}}(v)=sgn(uv-(c-u\delta)),

since then
h_{*}^{\textup{val}}(v)=sgn(uv-(c-u\delta))=sgn(u\cdot(v+\delta)-c)=h^{\textup{val}}_{u,c}(v)
as desired.

For subgradient queries
h^{\textup{sub}}_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}(\mathcal{M}(x),{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}})=sgn(\langle{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}},\mathcal{M}(x),{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}\rangle-c),
with
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n+d}
, denote by
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}_{n}
the vector of the first
n
entries of
\textstyle\bf u
, and by
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}_{d}
the vector of the last
d
entries of
\textstyle\bf u
. One may use

h_{*}^{\textup{sub}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}):=h_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}_{d},c-\langle{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}_{n},\mathcal{M}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}})\rangle}^{\textup{sub}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}})=sgn\Big(\langle{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}_{d},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}\rangle-(c-\langle{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}_{n},\mathcal{M}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}})\rangle)\Big),

since then we similarly have

h_{*}^{\textup{sub}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}})=sgn\Big(\langle{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}_{d},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}\rangle-(c-\langle{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}_{n},\mathcal{M}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}})\rangle)\Big)=sgn(\langle{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}},(\mathcal{M}(x),{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}})\rangle-c)=h^{\textup{sub}}_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}(\mathcal{M}(x),{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}})

as desired. For these hereditary queries, note that
h_{u,c-u\delta}^{\textup{val}}
and
h_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}_{d},c-\langle{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}_{n},\mathcal{M}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}})\rangle}^{\textup{sub}}
are indeed in
\mathcal{H}^{\textup{val}}_{0,d}
and
\mathcal{H}^{\textup{sub}}_{0,d}
, respectively, since
u\in\mathbb{R}
,
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}_{n}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, and
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf u$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf u$}}}_{d}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

We remark here that
\mathcal{H}^{bit}
, without the permitted “shifts” allowed in
\mathcal{H}^{bit^{*}}
is not hereditary, as it may not satisfy condition (
7
) for the function values for all
\delta
; however, any lower bounds obtained with
\mathcal{H}^{bit^{*}}
must also hold for
\mathcal{H}^{bit}
, since the former is a richer class of queries.

Definition of the adversary.

We will define
Adv-Cont+
analogously as in the full-information case, now receiving queries in
\mathcal{H}
according to the oracle setting considered.
Adv-Cont+
will be
\varepsilon
-hard for
\ell
rounds answering queries from
\mathcal{H}
, and after
\ell-1
rounds it commits to a single surviving function with optimal value
>OPT+\varepsilon
. As queries for general oracles using first-order information consist of a point
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}=({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
and a permissible query
h\in\mathcal{H}
, let us write
({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}},h)
for notational simplicity to denote such a query.

We describe here the behavior of
Adv-Cont+
in the general oracle case, and such that it satisfies the same invariant of Lemma
16
as in the full-information case, i.e. it is
\varepsilon
-hard for
\ell
rounds and only keeps a single surviving function with optimal value at least
OPT+\varepsilon
after
\ell
queries have been made.

Procedure 3
.

Adv-Cont+

Initialize set of survived functions
S_{0}=\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 1.52779pt\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{F}$}}_{cont}

For each round
t=1,2\ldots
:

1.

Receive query point
