({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}_{t},h_{t})\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathcal{H}
from the optimization algorithm.

2.

If
t\leq\ell-1
: Send
({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}_{t},h_{t})
to the adversary
Adv-Cont
, receiving back the answer
\alpha
. Obtain
S_{t}
by removing from
S_{t-1}
the functions
f
that are not consistent with this response under any first-order chart
\mathcal{G}
, namely
f
for which
h_{t}(f({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}_{t}))\not=\alpha
if
h_{t}\in\mathcal{H}^{\textup{val}}
, or for which there does not exist a
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}_{t}\in\partial f({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}_{t})
such that
h_{t}(f({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}_{t}),{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}_{t})=\alpha
if
h_{t}\in\mathcal{H}^{\textup{sub}}
.

Send the response
\alpha
to the optimization algorithm.

3.

If
t=\ell
: Since
Adv-Cont
is
\varepsilon
-hard for
\ell-1
rounds, there is a finite collection of functions
\{f_{1},...,f_{k}\}\subset S_{t-1}\cap\mathcal{F}_{cont}
that do not share an
\varepsilon
-solution. Define their pointwise maxima
f_{\max}=\max\{f_{1},...,f_{k}\}
and set
S_{t+k}=\{f_{\max}\}
, for all
k=0,1,2...
.

Set the value
v_{t}
to be
f_{\max}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}_{t})
and set
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}_{t}
to be a subgradient in
\partial f_{\max}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}_{t})
(consistent with what the first order chart
\mathcal{G}_{0}
gives for
f_{1},\ldots,f_{k}
at
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}_{t}
, if
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}_{t}
has been queried in an earlier round), and send the response
h_{t}(v_{t})
or
h_{t}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}_{t})
to the optimization algorithm, according to whether
h_{t}\in\mathcal{H}^{\textup{val}}
or
h_{t}\in\mathcal{H}^{\textup{sub}}
respectively.

4.

If
t>\ell
: Let
f_{\max}
be the only function in
S_{t-1}
.
If
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}_{t}
was queried in an earlier round
k
, answer
(v_{k},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}_{k})
. Otherwise,
as in the step above, set the value
v_{t}
to be
f_{\max}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}_{t})
and set
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}_{t}
to be any subgradient in
\partial f_{\max}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}_{t})
, and again send the appropriate response
h_{t}(v_{t})
or
h_{t}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}_{t})
to the optimization algorithm.

Proving that this
Adv-Cont+
satisfies the invariant from Lemma
16
follows exactly the same steps as in the full-information case. Hence, using this
Adv-Cont+
and the same family of functions
\psi_{\texttt{F}}
from (
5
), we will be able to construct
Adv-MI
for this general oracle case to satisfy a version of Invariant
1
, slightly modified for this general case to refine what we mean by functions being consistent with responses given to the more general queries. To achieve this, let
Adv-MI
operate according to the following procedure.

Procedure 4
.

Adv-MI

Instantiate a copy of
Adv-Cont+
on each fiber
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\in\{0,1\}^{n}
, and let
S({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}})
denote the set of surviving functions maintained by this copy, initialized to
\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{cont}
.

Set
\mathbf{M}_{x}:=3MR\cdot sgn({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}-0.5\cdot\mathbf{1})
, for any
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\in\{0,1\}^{n}
, as in (
2
). For each round

:

1.

receives the query

from the algorithm. Set

.

2.

Send the function value threshold query that answers“is
f_{r({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}_{t})}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}_{t})\leq OPT+\delta
?” to the adversary

of the closest fiber

If

and

, and send the query

Adv-Cont+
of the closest fiber

, which then returns some answer

returns

as its response to the original query.

Invariant 2
.

,

of current surviving functions

If the answer is no, then Lemma

has value

\displaystyle\psi_{\texttt{F}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf (9)

and for its subgradient we have

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}\in\partial\hat{f}_{r({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}_{t})}\implies(\mathbf{M}_{r({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}_{t})},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}})\in\partial\psi_{\texttt{F}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}_{t},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}_{t}),

(10)

by Lemma

).

, and so

, with
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}_{\psi}\in\partial\psi_{\texttt{F}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}_{t},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}_{t})

for choices

of the surviving functions on the fibers are consistent with the responses given by

with permissible queries

from Invariant

.

We now prove that
Adv-MI
is
\varepsilon
-hard for

rounds, thus proving Theorem
7
in the general case.
Since
Adv-MI
makes at most
2
queries to
Adv-Cont+
in every round (Step 2) of Procedure
4
, if the optimization algorithm runs for fewer than
2^{n-1}\cdot\ell
iterations, there is a fiber
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*}\in\{0,1\}
where
Adv-MI
sent at most
\ell-1
hereditary queries to the adversary
Adv-Cont+
of the fiber
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*}
.
Thus, by the guarantee of the latter (Item 2 of Lemma
16
), the surviving set
S({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*})
has some finite collection of functions
f^{1}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*}},...,f^{k}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*}}
with no common
\varepsilon
-approximate solution, and the remainder of the proof follows as in the full-information case, by considering
\psi_{\texttt{F}^{1}},...,\psi_{\texttt{F}^{k}}
that have
f^{1}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*}},...,f^{k}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*}}
on the fiber
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*}
, and some functions with optimal value greater than
OPT+\varepsilon
on all the other fibers.

3
Proof of Theorem
9

To demonstrate Theorem
9
, we need to introduce the idea of
information memory
of any query strategy/algorithm.

Definition 22
.

A first-order query strategy with information memory comprises three functions:

1.

\phi_{\textup{query}}:\{0,1\}^{*}\to[-R,R]^{n}\times[-R,R]^{d}

2.

\phi^{\textup{sep}}_{\textup{update}}:\left(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)\times\{0,1\}^{*}\to\{0,1\}^{*}

3.

\phi^{\textup{val}}_{\textup{update}}:\mathbb{R}\times\{0,1\}^{*}\to\{0,1\}^{*}

4.

\phi^{\textup{sub}}_{\textup{update}}:\left(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)\times\{0,1\}^{*}\to\{0,1\}^{*}
,

where
\{0,1\}^{*}
denotes the set of all binary strings (finite sequences over
\{0,1\}
), including the empty string.

Given access to a first-order chart
\mathcal{G}
, the query strategy maintains an information memory
r_{k}
at every iteration
k\geq 0
, which is a finite length binary string in
\{0,1\}^{*}
, with
r_{0}
initialized as the empty string. At every iteration
k=1,2,\ldots
, the query strategy computes
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}_{k}:=\phi_{query}(r_{k-1})
and updates its memory using either
r_{k}=\phi^{\textup{sep}}_{\textup{update}}\left({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}^{\textup{sep}}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}_{k}}(\widehat{f},\widehat{C}),r_{k-1}\right)
,
r_{k}=\phi^{\textup{val}}_{\textup{update}}\left({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}^{\textup{val}}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}_{k}}(\widehat{f},\widehat{C}),r_{k-1}\right)
or
r_{k}=\phi^{\textup{sub}}_{\textup{update}}\left({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}^{\textup{sub}}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}_{k}}(\widehat{f},\widehat{C}),r_{k-1}\right)
, where
(\widehat{f},\widehat{C})
is the unknown true instance. After finitely many iterations, the query strategy does a final computation based on its information memory and reports an
\varepsilon
-approximate solution, i.e., there is a final function
\phi_{\textup{fin}}:\{0,1\}^{*}\to{\mathbb{Z}}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

The information memory complexity of an algorithm for an instance is the maximum length of its information memory
r_{k}
over all iterations
k
during the processing of this instance.

The following proposition allows us to relate the information memory complexity of first-order algorithms with information complexity under access to a general binary oracle using first-order information.

Proposition 23
.

Let
\mathcal{G}
be a first-order chart. For any first-order query strategy
\mathcal{A}
with information memory that uses
\mathcal{G}
, there exists a query strategy
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
using the general binary oracle based on
\mathcal{G}
, such that for any instance
(f,C)
, if
\mathcal{A}
stops after
T
iterations with information memory complexity
Q
,
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
stops after making at most
Q\cdot T
oracle queries.

Conversely, for any query strategy
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
using the general binary oracle based on
\mathcal{G}
, there exists a first-order query strategy
\mathcal{A}
with information memory such that for any instance
(f,C)
, if
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
stops after
T
iterations,
\mathcal{A}
stops after making at most
T
iterations with information memory complexity at most
T
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a first-order query strategy with information memory. We can simulate
\mathcal{A}
by the query strategy whose queries are precisely the bits of the information memory state
r_{k}
at each iteration
k
of
\mathcal{A}
.
More formally, the query is
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}=\phi_{\textup{query}}(r_{k-1})
and
h(\cdot)=(\phi^{\textup{sep}}_{\textrm{update}}(\cdot,r_{k-1}))_{i}
,
h(\cdot)=(\phi^{\textup{val}}_{\textrm{update}}(\cdot,r_{k-1}))_{i}
, or
h(\cdot)=(\phi^{\textup{sub}}_{\textrm{update}}(\cdot,r_{k-1}))_{i}
, depending on which type of query was made, where
i
indexes different bits of the corresponding binary string.

Conversely, given a query strategy
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
based on the general binary oracle, we can simulate it with a first-order query strategy with information memory where in each iteration, we simply append the new bit queried by
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
to the current state of the memory.
∎

We need the following result derived from Marsden et al.
[
11
]
on information memory complexity.

Theorem 24
.

[
11
, Theorem 1]
For every
\delta\in[0,1/4]
, there is a class of instances
\mathcal{I}\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{n,d,R,\rho,M}
, where
n=0
, and a first-order chart
\mathcal{G}
such that any first-order query strategy with information memory must have either
d^{1.25-\delta}
information memory complexity (in the worst case) or make at least
\tilde{\Omega}(d^{1+\frac{4}{3}\delta})
iterations (in the worst case).

Proof of Theorem
9
.

In the case when
n=0
, we can set
\delta=\frac{3}{28}
in Theorem
24
to obtain that any first-order query strategy uses either
d^{8/7}
information memory or makes at least
\tilde{\Omega}(d^{8/7})
iterations. Using the second part of Proposition
23
, we obtain the lower bound of
\tilde{\Omega}(d^{8/7})
on the number of queries made by any query strategy using the general binary oracle based on
\mathcal{G}
.

Applying Theorem
7
enables us to extend the bound to the mixed-integer scenario (
n>0
). Further, by integrating this with Corollary
8
, we can obtain the desired bound.
∎

4
Proof of Theorems
10
and
11

We will use
B_{\infty}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf p$}}},\delta)
to denote the
\ell_{\infty}
ball of radius
\delta
centered at
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf p$}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
, i.e.,
B_{\infty}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf p$}}},\delta)=\{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}:\|{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf p$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf p$}}}\|_{\infty}\leq\delta\}
. Recall that we consider the subclass of instances
(f,C)\in\mathcal{I}_{n,d,R,\rho,M}
such that the fiber containing the optimal solution also contains a point that is
\rho
-deep in
C
, that is: if
({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}^{*})\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
is an optimal solution for this instance, then there is a point
({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*},\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}})
such that the full-dimensional ball
B_{\infty}(({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*},\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}}),\rho)
is contained in
C
. We use
\mathcal{I}_{n,d,R,\rho,M}^{deep}
to denote this subclass of instances. We will use
C_{-\rho}:=\{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}\in C:B_{\infty}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}},\rho)\subseteq C\}
to denote the set of all
\rho
-deep points in
C
.

Our strategy for proving Theorems
10
and
11
is to: 1) solve the the problems using approximate subgradients/separating hyperplanes; 2) use bit queries/inner product sign queries to construct such approximations.

For the first item, we use an algorithm designed by Oertel
[
13
]
(see also
[
3
]
)
based on the concept of a
centerpoint
: this is a point in the convex set where every halfspace supported on it cuts off a significant (mixed-integer) volume of the set.
The algorithm maintains an outer relaxation
P
of the feasible region
C
in every iteration, and repeatedly applies separation or subgradient-based cuts through the centerpoint of
P
. The assumption that the feasible region contains a ball (in the optimal fiber) establishes a volume lower bound that essentially limits the number of iterations of the algorithm. While the original algorithm in
[
13
,
3
]
uses exact separation/subgradient oracles, we show, not surprisingly, that approximate ones suffice. To prove Theorem
11
, we employ a similar approach. However, due to the continuous nature of the setting, we can obtain a better upper bound compared to Theorem
10
by applying a stronger bound on the centerpoints from Grünbaum
[
9
]
.

The next item is to construct approximate separation/subgradient oracles by making only a limited number of binary queries on the separating hyperplanes and/or subgradients. In case of bit queries
\mathcal{H}^{\textup{bit}}
this can be easily done by querying enough bits of the latter. The case of inner product sign queries
\mathcal{H}^{\textup{dir}}
, where we can pick a direction
\textstyle\bf a
and ask “Is
\langle{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf a$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf a$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}\rangle\geq 0
?” for the subgradient or separating hyperplane
\textstyle\bf g
, is more interesting. It boils down to approximating the vector
\textstyle\bf g
(subgradient/separating hyperplane) using few such queries.
2
2

2

This is related to (actively) learning the linear classifier whose normal is given by
\textstyle\bf g

[
1
]
. These methods can perhaps be adapted to our setting, but we present a different and self-contained statement and proof. See the discussion at the end of Section
4.2
.

To formalize the first item, we begin by defining three approximate oracles as follows.

Definition 25
.

We have the following:

•

An
\varepsilon
-approximate separation oracle

\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}}^{\textup{sep}}
is such that
\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}}^{\textup{sep}}_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}}(f,C)={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf 0$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf 0$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf 0$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf 0$}}}
iff
\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}
belongs to
C
, and otherwise the cut
\langle\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}}^{\textup{sep}}_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}}(f,C),{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}\rangle\leq\langle\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}}^{\textup{sep}}_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}}(f,C),\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}\rangle
is valid for all
\varepsilon
-deep points
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}\in C_{-\varepsilon}
.

•

An
\varepsilon
-approximate value cut oracle

\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}}^{\textup{sub}}
is such that for every
\textstyle\bf z
such that
\langle\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}}^{\textup{sub}}_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}}(f,C),{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}\rangle\geq\langle\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}}^{\textup{sub}}_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}}(f,C),\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}\rangle
, we have
f({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}})\geq f(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}})-\varepsilon
.

•

An
\varepsilon
-approximate value comparison oracle
is such that for every function
f:[-R,R]^{n+d}\rightarrow[-U,U]
and every pair of points
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}^{\prime}
we obtain the answer to the query “Is
f({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}})\leq f({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}^{\prime})+\varepsilon
?”.

Then the first item can be formalized as the following.

Theorem 26
.

There exists an algorithm that, for any
M,R>0
,
0<\varepsilon\leq MR
and
\rho>0
, can report an
\varepsilon
-approximate solution for every instance in
\mathcal{I}_{n,d,R,\rho,M}^{deep}
, using at most

O\bigg(2^{n}(n+d)d\log\bigg(\frac{MR}{\min\{\rho,1\}\varepsilon}\bigg)\bigg)

oracle calls, given access to any
\rho^{\prime}
-approximate separation oracle,
\varepsilon^{\prime}
-approximate value cut oracle, and
\varepsilon^{\prime}
-approximate value comparison oracle with
\rho^{\prime}=\frac{\varepsilon^{\prime}\rho}{4MR}
and
\varepsilon^{\prime}=\frac{\varepsilon}{6}
.

For the continuous setting with
n=0
, the bound can be improved to

O\bigg(d\log\bigg(\frac{MR}{\min\{\rho,1\}\varepsilon}\bigg)\bigg).

We postpone the proof of Theorem
26
to Section
4.1
.

The next lemma shows that one can implement the approximate oracles from Definition
25
using bit queries and inner product sign queries.

Lemma 27
.

Consider a first-order chart
\mathcal{G}
. Let
f:\mathbb{R}^{n+d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be a convex
M
-Lipschitz function taking values in
[-U,U]
, and
C\subseteq[-R,R]^{n+d}
a convex set.

Then for every pair of points
\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}},\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}^{\prime}\in[-R,R]^{n+d}
, we can obtain an
\varepsilon
-approximate separation oracle vector
\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}}^{\textup{sep}}_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}}(f,C)
, an
\varepsilon
-approximate value cut vector
\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}}^{\textup{sub}}_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}}(f,C)
, and an
\varepsilon
-approximate value comparison between
\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}
and
\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}^{\prime}
using either a sequence of bit queries from
\mathcal{H}^{\textup{bit}}
, or a sequence of inner product sign queries from
\mathcal{H}^{\textup{dir}}
, on the separating hyperplane
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}^{\textup{sep}}_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}}(f,C)
, the subgradient
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}^{\textup{sub}}_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}}(f,C)
and the function value
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}^{\textup{val}}_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}}(f,C)
. The number of required queries to implement the approximate oracles is
O\left((n+d)\log\frac{(n+d)R}{\varepsilon}\right)
,
O\left((n+d)\log\frac{(n+d)MR}{\varepsilon}\right)
and
O\left(\log\frac{U}{\varepsilon}\right)
respectively, for both
\mathcal{H}^{\textup{bit}}
and
\mathcal{H}^{\textup{dir}}
.

The proof of Lemma
27
is deferred to Section
4.2
.

Proof.

Theorems
10
and
11
follow from Theorem
26
and Lemma
27
.
∎

4.1
Proof of Theorem
26

We first describe the centerpoint algorithm for convex optimization due to Oertel
[
13
]
(see also
[
3
]
). Let the
mixed-integer volume
of a (Borel) set
U\in\mathbb{R}^{n+d}
be
\mu(U):=\sum_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{n}}\vol_{d}(U\cap(\{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}))
, where
\vol_{d}
is the
d
-dimensional Lebesgue measure. The following notion is the main element of the algorithm.

Theorem 28

(Mixed-integer centerpoint
[
13
,
3
]
)
.

For any compact convex set
C\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n+d}
, there is a point
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}\in C\cap({\mathbb{Z}}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
(called a
mixed-integer centerpoint
) such that for every halfspace
H
with
\textstyle\bf z
on its boundary, we have
\mu(C\cap H)\geq\frac{1}{2^{n}(d+1)}\,\mu(C)
.

Algorithm
1
below is the centerpoint-based algorithm for solving mixed-integer convex optimization problems from
[
13
]
, restated in terms of approximate separation (
\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}}^{\textup{sep}}_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}}(f,C)
), value cut (
\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}}^{\textup{sub}}_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}}(f,C)
) and value comparison oracles.

Algorithm 1

Centerpoints

1.

Initialize the version set
P_{0}:=[-R,R]^{n+d}
and the collection of feasible points
F=\emptyset
. For iterations
t=0,\ldots,T-1
:

(a)

Let
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}_{t}\in P_{t}\cap({\mathbb{Z}}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
be a mixed-integer centerpoint of
P_{t}
given by Lemma
28
.

(b)

If the
\rho^{\prime}
-approximate separation oracle says that
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}_{t}
is infeasible for
C
, add the cut
\langle\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}}^{\textup{sep}}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}_{t}}(f,C),{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}\rangle\leq\langle\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}}^{\textup{sep}}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}_{t}}(f,C),{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}_{t}\rangle
to
P_{t}
, namely set
P_{t+1}=P_{t}\cap\{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}:\langle\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}}^{\textup{sep}}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}_{t}}(f,C),{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}\rangle\leq\langle\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}}^{\textup{sep}}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}_{t}}(f,C),{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}_{t}\rangle\}

(c)

Else, add
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}_{t}
to the set of feasible solutions
F
, and add the cut from the
\varepsilon^{\prime}
-approximate value cut oracle, namely set
P_{t+1}=P_{t}\cap\{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}:\langle\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}}^{\textup{sub}}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}_{t}}(f,C),{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}\rangle\leq\langle\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}}^{\textup{sub}}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}_{t}}(f,C),{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}_{t}\rangle\}
.

2.

Finally, return a point
\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}
from
F
that has approximately the minimum value among all solutions in
F
, namely such that
f(\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}})\leq\min_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}\in F}f({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}})+\varepsilon^{\prime}
. This can be accomplished by asking
|F|-1
queries to the
\varepsilon^{\prime}
-approximate value comparison oracle.

To analyze this algorithm we need the following technical lemma regarding deep points in instances in
\mathcal{I}_{n,d,R,\rho,M}^{deep}
.

Lemma 29
.

For every instance
(f,C)
in
\mathcal{I}_{n,d,R,\rho,M}^{deep}
, and for every
0<\varepsilon<2MR
, there is an
\varepsilon
-approximate solution
\textstyle\bf z
such that the ball
B_{\infty}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}},\frac{\varepsilon\rho}{2MR})
is contained in
C
.

Proof.

Let
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}^{*}=({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}^{*})
be an optimal solution for the instance, and let
\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}=({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}^{*},\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}})
be such that
B_{\infty}(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}},\rho)
is contained in
C
. For
\alpha=\frac{\varepsilon}{2MR}
, we claim that the point
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}:=(1-\alpha){\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}^{*}+\alpha\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}
has the desired properties. First, by convexity of
C
we have that the desired ball
B_{\infty}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}},\frac{\varepsilon\rho}{2MR})=(1-\alpha){\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}^{*}+\alpha B_{\infty}(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}},\rho)
is contained in
C
. In addition, since
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}^{*}=\alpha\cdot(0,\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}}-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}^{*})
and
f
is
M
-Lipschitz over the integer fibers, we have

f({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}})\,\leq\,f({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}^{*})+\alpha M\cdot\|{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}^{*}-\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}}\|_{\infty}\,\leq\,f({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}^{*})+\varepsilon,

where the last inequality uses that
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}^{*},\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf y$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf y$}}}}\in[-R,R]^{d}
and the definition of
\alpha
; so
\textstyle\bf z
is an
\varepsilon
-approximate solution. This concludes the proof.
∎

Proof of Theorem
26
.

We show that Algorithm
1
with number of iterations set as

T=2^{n}(n+d)(d+1)\ln\bigg(\frac{2R}{\min\{\rho^{\prime},1\}}\bigg)\in O\left(2^{n}(n+d)d\ln\bigg(\frac{MR}{\min\{\rho,1\}\varepsilon}\bigg)\right)

has the desired properties First, regarding the number of oracle queries performed: in each iteration it performs at most 2 approximate separation/value cut queries, and in Step 2 it performs
|F|-1\leq T
approximate value comparison queries. In total, the algorithm performs at most
3T
queries, giving the desired complexity.

Now we show that the algorithm returns an
\varepsilon
-optimal solution. For that, it suffices to show that for this value of
T
, the set of feasible solutions
F
contains an
\frac{\varepsilon}{2}
-optimal solution.

Using Lemma
29
, let
\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}
be an
\varepsilon^{\prime}
-approximate solution such that the ball
B_{\infty}(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}},\frac{\varepsilon^{\prime}\rho}{2MR})=B_{\infty}(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}},2\rho^{\prime})
is contained in
C
. Thus, the ball
B_{\infty}(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}},\rho^{\prime})
is contained in
C_{-\rho^{\prime}}
. Since the cut added to
P_{t}
, whether in Step (b) or (c), goes through the centerpoint
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}_{t}
, the mixed-integer volume of
P_{t}
is reduced by a factor of at least
(1-\frac{1}{2^{n}(d+1)})
in each iteration (to simplify the notation let
\alpha:=\frac{1}{2^{n}(d+1)}
). The definition of
T
shows that the last set
P_{T}
has mixed-integer volume at most

\displaystyle(1-\alpha)^{T}\mu(P_{0})=(1-\alpha)^{T}(2R)^{n+d}\leq e^{-T\alpha}(2R)^{n+d}\leq\left(\min\{\rho^{\prime},1\}\right)^{n+d}\leq\left(\min\{\rho^{\prime},1\}\right)^{d}.

(11)

Let
X
be the intersection of
B_{\infty}(\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}},\rho^{\prime})
with the mixed-integer fiber containing
\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}
.
X
has the same structure as an
\ell_{\infty}
ball of radius
\rho^{\prime}
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and thus has volume at least
(2\rho^{\prime})^{d}
, which is strictly bigger than the right-hand side of (
11
). This means that some mixed-integer point from
X
is cut off by one of the hyperplanes applied by the algorithm. However, such a hyperplane cannot be one added in Step (b), since
B_{\infty}(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}},\rho^{\prime})\subseteq C_{-\rho^{\prime}}
and the cuts in that step are valid for
C_{-\rho^{\prime}}
. Thus, there is an iteration
t
that added a Step (c) approximate value cut that cut off a point
\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}\in X
. Thus,
\langle\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}}^{\textup{sub}}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}_{t}}(f,C),\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}\rangle>\langle\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}}^{\textup{sub}}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}_{t}}(f,C),{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}_{t}\rangle
. Since this is an
\varepsilon^{\prime}
-approximate value cut, we get that
f(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}})\geq f({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}_{t})-\varepsilon^{\prime}
. Since
f
is
M
-Lipschitz on the fiber containing
\bar{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}
and
\tilde{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}
, and the
\ell_{\infty}
distance between
\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}
and
\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}
is at most
\rho^{\prime}
, we get

\displaystyle f({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}_{t})\leq f(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}})+\varepsilon^{\prime}\leq f(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}})+\rho^{\prime}M+\varepsilon^{\prime}\leq\textup{OPT}+2\varepsilon^{\prime}+\rho^{\prime}M\leq\textup{OPT}+\frac{\varepsilon}{2},

where the last inequality uses that
\rho^{\prime}=\frac{\varepsilon^{\prime}\rho}{4MR}\leq\frac{\varepsilon^{\prime}}{M}
and that
\varepsilon^{\prime}=\frac{\varepsilon}{6}
.

This shows that the set of feasible solutions
F
contains an
\frac{\varepsilon}{2}
-approximate solution, namely the above
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}_{t}
, as desired. This concludes the proof for the mixed-integer setting.

For the continuous setting with
n=0
, the improved bound follows from using an improved bound on centerpoints due to Grünbaum
[
9
]
. Specifically,
\alpha
in the left hand side of (
11
) can be taken to be
\frac{1}{e}
, where
e
is Euler’s constant.
∎

4.2
Proof of Lemma
27

The proof will be divided into two parts: first, we show how to obtain approximate oracles using bit queries, after which we show how to do the same using inner product sign queries.

Obtaining approximate oracles using bit queries.

Since
f
is
M
-Lipschitz with respect to the
\ell_{\infty}
norm, any subgradient has
\ell_{\infty}
norm at most
M
. Thus, letting
\varepsilon^{\prime}:=\frac{\varepsilon}{2(n+d)R}
, we will query the sign and the bits indexed by the integers
\lceil\log M\rceil,\lceil\log M\rceil-1,\ldots,-\lfloor\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{\prime}}\rfloor
of each coordinate of
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}^{\textup{sub}}_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}}(f,C)
(nonnegative integers index the bits before the decimal, and negative integers index the bits after the decimal in the binary representation). This can be done by querying the bits of
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}^{\textup{sub}}_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}}(f,C)
for a total of
(n+d)(\log\frac{M}{\varepsilon^{\prime}}+2)
queries – for each coordinate, one queries
\log M+\log(\frac{1}{\varepsilon})+1
bits for the desired precision and one additional bit for the sign. This gives a vector
\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}}^{\textup{sub}}_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}}(f,C)

such that
\|{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}^{\textup{sub}}_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}}(f,C)-\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}}^{\textup{sub}}_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}}(f,C)\|_{\infty}\leq\sum_{i>\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{\prime}}}\frac{1}{2^{i}}\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}
.

Then
\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}}^{\textup{sub}}_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}}(f,C)
is an
\varepsilon
-approximate value cut. Let
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}:={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}^{\textup{sub}}_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}}(f,C)
and
\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}}:=\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}}^{\textup{sub}}_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}}(f,C)
to simplify notation. For every
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}\in[-R,R]^{n+d}
such that
\langle\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}\rangle\geq\langle\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}},\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}\rangle
we have by convexity of
f

\displaystyle f({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}})-f(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}})\geq\langle{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}-\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\langle\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}-\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}\rangle}_{\geq 0}+\langle{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}-\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}-\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}\rangle

\displaystyle\geq-\|{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}-\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}}\|_{\infty}\cdot\|{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}-\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\geq-2\varepsilon^{\prime}(n+d)R\,=\,-\varepsilon,

(12)

where the second inequality follows from Hölder’s inequalty, and so
\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}}
has the desired property.

For
\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}}^{\textup{sep}}_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}}(f,C)
, recall that by assumption the separating vector
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}^{\textup{sep}}_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}}(f,C)
has unit length, and hence
\|{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}^{\textup{sep}}_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}}(f,C)\|_{\infty}\leq 1
. Then querying the sign and the bits indexed by
0,-1,\ldots,-\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{\prime}}
of each coordinate of
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}^{\textup{sep}}_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}}(f,C)
we obtain a vector
\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}}^{\textup{sep}}_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}}(f,C)
such that
\|{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}^{\textup{sep}}_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}}(f,C)-\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}}^{\textup{sep}}_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}}(f,C)\|_{\infty}\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}
.

We claim that
\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}}^{\textup{sep}}_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}}(f,C)
is an
\varepsilon
-approximate separation oracle, namely the inequality
\langle\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}}^{\textup{sep}}_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}}(f,C),{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}\rangle\leq\langle\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}}^{\textup{sep}}_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}}(f,C),\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}\rangle
holds for all
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}\in C_{-\varepsilon}
. As before, to simplify the notation we use
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}:={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}^{\textup{sep}}_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}}(f,C)
and
\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}}:=\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}}^{\textup{sep}}_{\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}}(f,C)
. For every
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}\in[-R,R]^{n+d}
we have

\displaystyle\langle\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}\rangle=\langle{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}\rangle+\langle\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}}-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}\rangle\leq\langle{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}\rangle+\|\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}}-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}\|_{\infty}\cdot\|{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}\|_{1}\leq\langle{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}\rangle+\varepsilon^{\prime}R(n+d)=\langle{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}\rangle+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}.

(13)

Now we claim that for every point
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}\in C_{-\varepsilon}
we have
\langle{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}\rangle\leq\langle{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}},\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}\rangle-\varepsilon
: since the inequality
\langle{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf x$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf x$}}}\rangle\leq\langle{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}},\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}\rangle
is valid for the ball
B({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}},\varepsilon)\subseteq C
, we have

\displaystyle\langle{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}},\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}\rangle\geq\max_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf w$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf w$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf w$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf w$}}}\in B(0,\varepsilon)}\langle{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}+{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf w$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf w$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf w$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf w$}}}\rangle=\langle{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}\rangle+\varepsilon,

(14)

proving the claim. Finally, we claim that
\langle{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}},\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}\rangle\leq\langle\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}},\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}\rangle+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}
:

\displaystyle\langle{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}},\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}\rangle-\langle\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}},\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}\rangle=\langle{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}-\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}},\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}\rangle\leq\|{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}-\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}}\|_{\infty}\cdot\|\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}\|_{1}\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}R(n+d)=\frac{\varepsilon}{2}.

(15)

Combining inequalities (
13
)-(
15
) proves that the cut
\langle\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}\rangle\leq\langle\hat{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}},\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}\rangle
is valid for
C_{-\varepsilon}
.

To obtain the
\varepsilon
-approximate value comparison oracle, since
f
takes values in
[-U,U]
, it suffices to probe the sign plus
\log\frac{2U}{\varepsilon}
bits of
f(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}})
and
f(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}^{\prime})
to approximate each of the values within
\pm\frac{\varepsilon}{2}
, in which case we can decide whether
f(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}})\leq f(\bar{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf z$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf z$}}}}^{\prime})+\varepsilon
or not. This concludes the proof when using bit queries.

Obtaining approximate oracles using inner product sign queries.
This proof largely follows the same steps as for the first part, except for the need for the following result, which may be of independent interest.

Lemma 30
.

For any
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
and any vector
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\bf g$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\bf g$}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, using
O(d\log\frac{d}{\varepsilon})
inner product sign queries one can obtain a unit-length vector
